These new oral anticoagulants have predictable pharmacology, less variability in anticoagulant effect and fewer drug and food interactions than warfarin, allowing unmonitored and fixed dosing, which renders their use appealing. The remaining limitations of currently available new oral anticoagulants include their dependence on renal and hepatic clearance, and the lack of an antidote, which is problematic in bleeding patients and those requiring urgent surgery. Betrixaban was tested in Phase II studies in orthopedic thromboprophylaxis (EXPERT) and atrial fibrillation (EXPLORE-Xa), and is being evaluated in a Phase III trial of extended thromboprophylaxis in medical patients (APEX). This article details the pharmacology, preclinical and clinical development of betrixaban.

To address the hypothesis that all APOBEC3 proteins have access to genomic DNA, we analyzed the localization of the APOBEC3 proteins during mitosis. We show that APOBEC3A, APOBEC3C and APOBEC3H are excluded from condensed chromosomes, but become cell-wide during telophase. However, APOBEC3B, APOBEC3D, APOBEC3F and APOBEC3G are excluded from chromatin throughout mitosis. Both structural motifs as well as size may be factors in regulating chromatin exclusion. Deaminase activity was not dependent on cell cycle phase. We also analyzed APOBEC3-induced cell cycle perturbations as a measure of each enzyme's capacity to inflict genomic DNA damage. AID, APOBEC3A and APOBEC3B altered the cell cycle profile, and, unexpectedly, APOBEC3D also caused changes. We conclude that several APOBEC3 family members have access to the nuclear compartment and can impede the cell cycle, most likely through DNA deamination and the ensuing DNA damage response. This process ensures that the DNA methylation status is preserved in the daughter cells after DNA replication. Other enzymes, such as DNMT2 and DNMT3, are also involved in DNA methylation, but their roles are more related to de novo methylation and regulation rather than maintenance.

=== idx: 283 ===
Gold supporting: It has been previously shown that disruption of RAD26 in yeast strain W303-1B results in a strain that is deficient in transcription-coupled repair (TCR), the preferential repair of the transcribed strand of an expressed gene over the non-transcribed strand and the rest of the genome.
LLM supporting:  The provided context does not explicitly mention the specific gene strand targeted by transcription-coupled repair (TCR). However, based on the general understanding of TCR, it primarily targets the non-template (non-coding) strand of DNA. This is because TCR is a mechanism that specifically recognizes and corrects errors in the DNA template strand, which is transcribed into mRNA. The non-template strand, also known as the coding strand, is not directly involved in the transcription process but is used as a reference for the correct sequence of the transcribed mRNA. Therefore, when a mutation or error occurs in the DNA template strand, TCR is activated to repair it, ensuring that the resulting mRNA is accurate. This targeted approach allows TCR to efficiently correct transcriptional errors, maintaining the fidelity of gene expression.

ROUGE-1: 0.3444 ROUGE-2: 0.0787 ROUGE-L: 0.1667

Thus, we can see which genes from the microsporidia score most closely based on the LPI with other eukaryotic organisms. In this analysis, we calculated the LPI for each gene from the genomes of 7 Microsporidia, Antonospora locustae, Enterocytozoon bieneusi, Encephalitozoon cuniculi, Encephalitozoon intestinalis, Nosema bombycis, Nosema ceranae, and Nematocida parisii, to analyze the genetic relationships between Microsporidia and other species. It was found that many (91%) genes were most closely correlated with genes from other microsporidial genomes and had the highest mean LPI (0.985), indicating a monophyletic origin of the Microsporidia. This is distinct from traditional androgen deprivation therapies, which primarily act on the testes to reduce testosterone production. Orteronel's ability to suppress androgen synthesis at the level of the prostate and other androgen-dependent tissues makes it a promising candidate for the treatment of prostate cancer.

LLM supporting:  In patients with Allan-Herndon-Dudley syndrome (AHDS), the primary alteration is in the transport of thyroid hormones, specifically thyroxine (T4) and triiodothyronine (T3), across the blood-brain barrier. This is due to mutations in the MCT8 gene, which encodes the monocarboxylate thyroid hormone transporter. The clinical presentation often includes normal serum T3 levels, but there can be variations in other thyroid hormone concentrations. Specifically, some patients may have low free T4 (FT4) levels, while others may have normal or even elevated T3 levels. The key issue is the impaired delivery of thyroid hormones to the brain, leading to neurological symptoms such as mental retardation, hypotonia, and ataxia, regardless of the peripheral thyroid hormone levels. Therefore, the most accurate statement is that the concentration of thyroid hormones that are not properly transported to the brain is altered, particularly affecting the availability of T4 and T3 in the central nervous system.

It is important to note that the primary issue is not with the production or release of thyroid hormones but rather with their transport into cells, where they are needed for proper growth and development, particularly in the brain. Therefore, the alteration in hormone concentrations is more accurately described as a defect in thyroid hormone utilization rather than a deficiency in the hormones themselves.

=== idx: 817 ===
Gold supporting: Pendred syndrome consists in sensorineural deafness, goiter and impaired thyroid hormone synthesis while in EVA thyroid function seems to be preserved.
LLM supporting:  The key hormone abnormalities characteristic to Pendred syndrome, as supported by the context, include: 1. **Thyroid Hormone Imbalance**: Patients with Pendred syndrome often exhibit hypothyroidism, which is characterized by low levels of thyroid hormones (T3 and T4) and high levels of thyroid-stimulating hormone (TSH). 2. **Elevated TSH Levels**: The elevation of TSH is a common finding due to the impaired thyroid function. 3. **Increased Thyroglobulin (Tg) Levels**: There is an increase in thyroglobulin, a protein produced by the thyroid gland, which is indicative of the body's attempt to compensate for the reduced thyroid hormone production. 4. **Positive Perchlorate Discharge Test**: This test is often positive, reflecting a defect in the thyroid's ability to retain iodine, which is essential for the synthesis of thyroid hormones. 5. **Enlarged Thyroid Volume**: An increase in thyroid volume, as detected by thyroid echography, is another characteristic feature. These abnormalities collectively indicate a dysfunction in the thyroid gland, leading to issues in the production and regulation of thyroid hormones. The presence of these hormonal imbalances, particularly hypothyroidism, is a hallmark of Pendred syndrome.

The rationale for this combination is to enhance the therapeutic effect of levodopa by inhibiting its peripheral metabolism, thus increasing the amount of levodopa available for conversion to dopamine in the brain. This is particularly relevant in the treatment of Parkinson's disease, where the goal is to increase the levels of dopamine, which is a neurotransmitter that plays a crucial role in motor control and other functions. The specific mechanism by which benzerazide (benserazide) achieves this is by blocking the peripheral enzymes that break down levodopa, thereby allowing more of it to reach the central nervous system where it can be converted to dopamine.
Coverage: 0

To address the hypothesis that all APOBEC3 proteins have access to genomic DNA, we analyzed the localization of the APOBEC3 proteins during mitosis. We show that APOBEC3A, APOBEC3C and APOBEC3H are excluded from condensed chromosomes, but become cell-wide during telophase. AID, APOBEC3A and APOBEC3B altered the cell cycle profile, and, unexpectedly, APOBEC3D also caused changes. We conclude that several APOBEC3 family members have access to the nuclear compartment and can impede the cell cycle, most likely through DNA deamination and the ensuing DNA damage response. === idx: 932 ===
Gold supporting: Transcription-coupled repair (TCR) is generally observed as more rapid or more efficient removal of certain types of DNA damage from the transcribed strands of expressed genes compared with the nontranscribed strands.
LLM supporting:  The context provided does not explicitly mention the specific gene strand targeted by transcription-coupled repair (TCR). However, based on the established understanding of TCR, it primarily targets the non-template (or coding) strand of DNA. This is because TCR is a subpathway of nucleotide excision repair (NER) that specifically addresses DNA damage blocking RNA polymerase during transcription. The damage, which can include UV-induced pyrimidine dimers and other bulky adducts, occurs on the strand being transcribed, leading to the stalling of the RNA polymerase. TCR then removes the lesion, allowing transcription to resume.

LLM supporting:  The context provided does not explicitly mention the specific gene strand targeted by transcription-coupled repair (TCR). However, based on the established understanding of TCR, it primarily targets the non-template (or coding) strand of DNA. This is because TCR is a subpathway of nucleotide excision repair (NER) that specifically addresses DNA damage blocking RNA polymerase during transcription. The damage, which can include UV-induced pyrimidine dimers and other bulky adducts, occurs on the strand being transcribed, leading to the stalling of the RNA polymerase. TCR then removes the lesion, allowing transcription to resume.

=== idx: 994 ===
Gold supporting: Severe progressive obstructive cardiomyopathy and renal tubular dysfunction in Donohue syndrome with decreased insulin receptor autophosphorylation due to a novel INSR mutation.
LLM supporting:  The function of the insulin receptor (IR) is altered in patients with Donohue syndrome. Specifically, there is a severe decrease in the autophosphorylation of the insulin receptor, which is critical for its signaling activity. This impairment leads to the clinical manifestations observed in the syndrome, including the severe progressive hypertrophic obstructive cardiomyopathy (HOCM) and renal tubular dysfunction. The treatment with recombinant human insulin-like growth factor-1 (rhIGF-I) helps to partially reverse these conditions by bypassing the defective insulin receptor and directly activating the downstream signaling pathways that are crucial for normal cellular function and growth.
